199	      for p in $scope; do [ -d "$d/$p" ] && echo "$d/$p"; done
200	    fi
201	  done
202	  [ -d tests/contracts ] && echo tests/contracts
203	}
204	
205	# deptry checks what each package imports against what it declares.
206	dependencies() {
207	  local p scope
208	  if [ -z "$PACKAGES_DIR" ]; then
209	    run deptry . --no-ansi
210	    return
211	  fi
212	  if [ -n "$pkg" ]; then
213	    scope=$(pkg_scope "$pkg")
214	  else
215	    scope=$(cd "$PACKAGES_DIR" && for d in */pyproject.toml; do [ -f "$d" ] && dirname "$d"; done)
216	  fi
217	  for p in $scope; do
218	    echo "-> deptry $PACKAGES_DIR/$p" >&2
219	    (cd "$PACKAGES_DIR/$p" && uv run --frozen --quiet deptry . --no-ansi) || { rc=1; failed+=("deptry $PACKAGES_DIR/$p"); }
220	  done
221	}
222	
223	static_checks() {
224	  run ruff check --ignore-noqa .   # exceptions go in per-file-ignores, which is protected (CR-01)

I fixed the check script, committed it on `build/M1` as `365ee31`, recorded your decision on M1-T01, and restarted the build.

The fix is one line in `scripts/check.sh`'s `pkg_scope`:
```
rllab) echo "" ;;   # the package itself (what dt claim writes): empty scope = every test
```
An empty scope runs every test directory, which is the same as running with no `--pkg`. That's a tightening, not a loosening. `dt status --skill build-setup` shows no gaps. I couldn't run `bash -n` to check the script's syntax, because that needs a permission I don't have here, so the fix is untested until this gate run uses it.

A fresh agent is running M1-T01 now. Once it lands, the interface review runs.

M1-T15's tester escalated a contract change, so M1-T15 and M1-T04 are held. Its golden cases need values the frozen `agents.TabularAgent` doesn't expose:

<output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-connorott-plugins-dev-team-workspace-iteration-b1-rl9-incremental-A3-r1-with-skill-project/f85b4f9f-0417-40f7-abd3-1d2071e0cb12/tasks/a94e2b788cb05a64e.output</output-file>
<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "M1-T03 solo" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I built the run store in `src/rllab/runs/store.py`: `create_run`, `RunWriter` and `open_run` now write a run folder and read it back. `scripts/check.sh gate` passes, and the 7 new tests in `tests/unit/rllab/test_store.py` pass.

**How it works:**
- **Folder name:** `&lt;name&gt;-s&lt;seed&gt;-&lt;YYYYmmdd-HHMMSS&gt;`. If that name is taken, it adds `-1`, `-2` and so on, so it is always unique.
- **On `create_run`:** writes `settings.json`, a `curve.csv` with only its header, and `status` set to `running`.
- **Curve points:** each `add_point` is appended to `curve.csv` at once, so the curve survives an interrupt.
- **Checkpoints:** `checkpoint.npz` for a tabular agent, `checkpoint.pt` otherwise. A multi-team run uses `checkpoint-&lt;team&gt;.&lt;ext&gt;`.
- **Generations:** saved under `generations/&lt;team&gt;/&lt;index&gt;`, and the path is returned. `open_run` lists them in number order, so 10 comes after 9.
- **Games:** all recordings go into one compressed `games.npz`, with no pickled data.
- **On `finish`:** writes the status, then reads the folder back through `open_run`.

**Errors from `open_run`:**
- A missing folder or missing `settings.json` raises `FileNotFoundError` naming the run.
- An unreadable `games.npz` raises `ValueError` naming the file. Unreadable settings, a bad curve or an unknown status also raise `ValueError`.

**Choices I made where the contract was silent:**
- **Constructor:** I gave `RunWriter` an `__init__(path, settings)`, because the frozen interface only lists its methods.
- **Checkpoint extension:** whether a checkpoint is `.npz` or `.pt` depends on whether the agent is tabular.
- **Status file:** a run folder with no `status` file raises `FileNotFoundError` rather than being read as some default status.

DONE M1-T03 solo</result>

Proposed: add to agents.TabularAgent a 'value(obs: np.ndarray, action: int) -> float' (0 for unseen keys), and a pure function 'core.exploration_rate(exploration: Exploration, step: int) -> float' (or an 'epsilon' property plus a stated step counter). ABL-03 is testable as is."
    }
  ],
  "all_landed": false
}

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>`load_report_spec` now reads a report TOML and returns the `ClaimSpec` list plus the `ReportTargets`. A claim that names a player not in the file raises `ValueError`. `scripts/check.sh gate` passes, including the 13 new unit tests (`pytest tests/unit/rllab -k report_spec`).

Nothing in the repo defined the report file's layout, so I chose one, and it is set out in the module docstring:
- one `[players.&lt;name&gt;]` table per player, with `runs` or `baseline`, and optionally `team` and `vs` for predator-prey;
- one `[charts]` table with `baselines`, `ladder`, `ablations` and `crossplay`;
- one `[[claims]]` table per claim, with `name`, `a`, `b`, `direction`, and optionally `stretch` and `min_ratio`.

It also raises `ValueError` for an unknown key, a missing claim key or an unknown `direction`. A chart naming an unlisted player fails through the frozen `ReportTargets` check.

Run and chart paths are kept exactly as written, not resolved against the TOML's folder. That matches CLI use from the project root (`reports/smoke.toml` pointing at `runs/...`). If later tasks expect paths relative to the TOML file, this needs changing.

The tests cover reading the claims and targets, matching the `lab.ReportTargets` contract sample (`m1_snake.json`), files with no claims or charts, and each error path.

A second contract change came up, from the replay-viewer tester (M1-T20), so that task is now held too. The frozen `viz.Replay` has only `advance()` and `show()`, so its tests can't see:
- the frame, score and step currently drawn;
- the current speed (fps);
- whether it is paused;
- any way to send a key press without a window.

The tester proposes adding `fps`, `index` and `paused` attributes, a `current()` view of what's drawn, and `on_key(key)`.

It also flagged that TC-28 compares against `Snake.frame()`, but `envs.Snake` isn't in the task's Read list.

I started the convolutional-network tester (M1-T27) in the free slot. Six agents are running: M1-T12, T17, T18, T19, T26 and T27.

Three tasks are now held on two contract changes:
- M1-T15 and M1-T04, on `TabularAgent`;
- M1-T20, on `viz.Replay`.

I'll bring both to you together once the build drains.

<result>I implemented M1-T18: `eval_seeds` and `evaluate` in `/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/project/.claude/worktrees/agent-a98501f9c194c4f8d/src/rllab/evaluate/protocol.py` now do real work in place of the stubs. Unit tests are in `/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/project/.claude/worktrees/agent-a98501f9c194c4f8d/tests/unit/rllab/test_evaluate.py`, and `scripts/check.sh gate` passes (480 tests).

- **`eval_seeds`** returns `1_000_000 + 1000·train_seed + i`. It raises `ValueError` if `n` is over 1000 or `train_seed` is 1,000,000 or more.
- **`evaluate`** builds one environment and checks there is one agent per team (`ValueError` if not). It then plays one greedy game per held-out seed (`explore=False`, never calls `learn`). Each game's score is the chosen team's final `score:&lt;team&gt;`, and the first `record` games are kept as `GameRecording`s.

Every game is recorded while it is played and only the first `record` are kept. Unrecorded games still store their frames for a moment, which costs some time and memory. The 200-game intent test finishes well under the 60-second limit.

full text
M1-T18, held-out evaluation, landed. Progress is 14/31 tasks.

A third contract change came up, from the DQN ablation-switch tester (M1-T26), so M1-T26 is held too. It can't check TC-14 or ABL-01 through the frozen `DQNAgent`, which exposes only `act`, `learn`, `save` and `load`. Its tests need inspection points:
- the online and target networks;
- an update counter;
- the checkpoint key layout;
- which transitions each update sampled.

It also found two mismatches in its own materials:
- **Old switch names:** TC-14 and the plan still say `use_target`/`use_memory`, but AD-18 changed them to `ablation="no_target"`/`"no_memory"`. This is the plan-wording drift I mentioned when you chose to skip the Replan.
- **Missing declarations:** the task's packet doesn't declare the `Corridor` environment or the TC-14 transition generator that the tests use.

Six agents are running:
- replay game (M1-T12) and wire PL-02 (M1-T22)
- the scripted baseline (M1-T17) and tested claims (M1-T19) implementers
- the convolutional network (M1-T27) and interrupted-run (M1-T28) testers

Held on three contract changes:
- **`TabularAgent`:** M1-T15 and M1-T04
- **`viz.Replay`:** M1-T20
- **`DQNAgent`:** M1-T26

I'll bring them to you together once the build drains.

<result>I wrote the three TC-25 intent tests and `scripts/check.sh collect` passes. The steps-mismatch and seeds-mismatch tests fail the right way: `DID NOT RAISE ValueError`. The exact-match test already passes. That's expected, because `open_curve_runs` already opens the runs and just doesn't check them yet. `red_check` lists it without counting it as a failure.

- **Steps mismatch:** a full `dqn-compact` set (300,000 steps, seeds 0–4) against a `no_target` set with 200,000 steps must raise `ValueError` matching the fixture's `"steps"`.
- **Seeds mismatch:** the same full set against `no_target` seeds 0–3 must raise `ValueError` matching `"seeds"`.
- **Exact match:** no error, and `runs.open_run` is called once for each of the ten run folders.

Run sets, expected errors and names are all loaded from `tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-25/`. Each run's record is built from the `runs.RunRecord` and `core.RunSettings` contract samples. `runs.open_run` is replaced by a stand-in that serves those records by folder name.

Two assumptions, since the packet doesn't give `open_curve_runs`'s full signature or say how an ablation is matched to its full agent:
- **Call shape:** I call `open_curve_runs(targets)` with a `ReportTargets`, as in the entry sketch. I build it by decoding through the contract kit, so its field names come from the schema.
- **Pairing:** the full agent is in `ladder` and the ablation in `ablations`. They share agent, env and view, differ only in `ablation` (`none` vs `no_target`), and the ablation's name is the full agent's name plus `-no-target`. So the tests work whether the implementer pairs by name or by settings.

The M1 build has stopped. Everything still ready to start is held until you decide on the problems below. 18 of 31 tasks have landed, and two more have their tests written (M1-T27 and M1-T30). No agents are running.

**What landed:**
- the interface (M1-T01);
- all eleven spines (M1-T02, T03, T05–T12);
- six thicken tasks: Snake (M1-T13), the corridor (T14), the two baselines (T16, T17), evaluation (T18) and claims (T19);
- PL-02 wiring (M1-T22).

**Contract changes: four testers couldn't test against the frozen interface.**

| Task | Type | What the tests can't see | What the tester proposes |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-T15 (also holds M1-T04) | `agents.TabularAgent` | individual table values (TC-12); the exploration rate at a given step (TC-13) | `TabularAgent.value(obs, action)` and a pure function `core.exploration_rate(exploration, step)` |
| M1-T20 | `viz.Replay` | the frame being drawn, the fps, whether it's paused, and a way to press a key without a window | `fps`, `index` and `paused` attributes, `current()`, and `on_key(key)` |
| M1-T26 | `agents.DQNAgent` | the online and target networks, an update counter, the checkpoint layout, which transitions were sampled | named inspection points for each of those |
| M1-T29 | `reference` | the AD-03 adapter and the mapping to Stable-Baselines3 settings (TC-17) | freeze `reference.make_adapter(env)` and `reference.dqn_kwargs(settings)`, with samples |

**Plan drift:** the M1-T26 tester also found that TC-14 and the plan still use the old `use_target` and `use_memory` names. Its packet doesn't declare `Corridor` or the transition generator its tests use. The M1-T20 tester asks whether TC-28's expected frames should come from the `viz.Replay` sample, since `envs.Snake` isn't in that task's Read list. Both were filed as part of the escalations above.

**M1-T28 has no work left for an implementer.** It was filed as "needs a dependency", but none is missing: the training-loop spine (M1-T06) already handles an interrupted run. All 6 new tests pass at the task's base, so they can't start out failing the way the process requires. This filing is what is holding everything else back right now. The tester suggests landing the tests as regression coverage and closing M1-T28 with no implementer half.

**My recommendation:** the interface has landed now, so these contract changes need a re-freeze. Do them in one round:
1. **`architecture-design` Revise:** add all four sets of inspection points to the contract.
2. **`build-planning` Replan:**
   - add a re-freeze interface task;
   - correct the stale plan and TC-14 wording;
   - fix the M1-T26 and M1-T20 packet gaps;
   - close M1-T28 by landing its tests as regression coverage.
3. **A new go from you:** the Replan creates a new plan version, so the build needs your go again before it resumes.

You'd approve each step before I commit it. Shall I start step 1 and show you the draft?
